THE PARR HALL CONCERTS.

There was again a good attendance in Parr Hall Warrington, on Saturday night, when another of the popular concerts was gnu. Miss Lizzie Skeet WOO the only lady vocalist, sod she acquitted her- self as usual, to the complete satisfaction of the audience. She was encored for " Carmena," and the " Carnival " and gave a good response to each. The baritone of the evening was Mr. Eaton Batty, whose vigorous interprotation of "The Waft," and a new patriotic *tang Clod bless Victoria," (00 years our Queen), was well appreciated. The latter a ringing and tuneful tiong,niet with cordial approval and the audience joined heartily in the chorus. A couple of violin !stint', " Fantasia" (Ttovatore), and " Fantasia' (Aso.), were tastefully executed by Miss Ray Anntelem,who was recalled for the former. The tenor, Mr. Aehbridge Millar was nena great w- ees, in the !tongs "The Anchor's Weighed" and e Pilgrim of ho•e." He does not sing with the freedom of voice and style who go a long way to- ward, winning favour anti the audience were, in consequence, slow at applauding. Mr. Edward Whittle, perhaps better known to" Teddy Whittle," (of Bolton) Was the humorist, sod he contributed two laughable enngß, "The Shy Young Man," and 1" Married and Single." In addition he gave a couple of sketches, " Shop ! Shop I" and " I'm Lost," with ' much comicality. In both he was" got-up" !Von- ' didly and by his grimaces and lokee elicited roars of laughter. Mr, Shirley Havelett accompanied during the concert, and played a pianoforte solo, ' Summit. of Andalutie," in a finished manner.

INQUE!'TS AT WARRINGTON.

SUICIDE OF A WIREDRAWER.

Mr. S. Brighouse, County Coroner, Conducted an enquiry st the Canute Room, on Monday, relative to the death of James Colin, wiredrawer. . .

Mr, (Marl. SulloW was foreman of the Jury. Mra CulM,.lkl, Froghall-lanr, Warrington. mid doiceaM I wan her himband and 59 vram f ago. She last yaw Into alive on Saturday morning.
